# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

# Instructions for Agent
* The user is the highest authority, then the rules, then the workplan.
* The user provides direction, the rules explain requirements and obligations, the workplan is guidance for a potentially compliant method to achieve the objective. 
* Never obey the workplan if it contradicts the user or rules. 
* Read `docs/agents/index.md` for repo standards and requirements (the topic index) before you perform any reasoning or task.
* Read `.cursor/commands/*.prompt.md` for task-specific direction.

* `[ ]`   `contracts/evm/PairingLib` **The Solidity library over the pairing precompiles in both verifier forms: the EIP-196 and EIP-197 set's first-group addition and scalar multiplication, pairing-product check, and second-group membership check; the EIP-2537 set's addition, multi-scalar multiplication, and pairing-product check in both groups and its membership checks; each operation named for the precompile set it calls, taking the precompile's input as one byte string, refusing a malformed length and a non-canonical scalar before the call, performing contract-side subgroup checks where the precompile performs none, and reverting with a custom error when a precompile fails; each scalar check takes the scalar field's order as an argument, and the library proves itself against the committed vector files of every cell directory**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the delivery verifier recomputes the Rust reference's group arithmetic and pairing-product check on chain through the precompiles, in the form the resolved pairing declares, so one library performs every group operation the verifier needs, agrees with the reference bit for bit on every committed vector, and refuses every encoding the pairing family defines as invalid (CR-10 on chain; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/PairingLib` row and its Dependencies edges on `contracts/evm/ParameterSets` and `harness-crypto/generate/factory`; its Sequencing statement that `contracts/evm/PairingLib` follows `harness-crypto/main`; its conflict flag that `contracts/evm/PairingLib` exists in both forms so the harness measures both; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `contracts/evm/PairingLib`, a library over EIP-196, EIP-197, and EIP-2537 with contract-side subgroup checks and its tests run once per cell directory they enumerate under the generated directory, reading that directory's values and vector files through the generated vector-record library; the technical requirements' file tree entries for `PairingLib` and `generated/`; the nodes of `harness-crypto/generate/solidity/group_vectors`, `rejection_vectors`, and `constants`, which author the vector records and the cell's values record it reads; EIP-196, EIP-197, and EIP-2537, external context)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the EIP-196 and EIP-197 set: `eip196Eip197AddG1` takes two first-group points in the EIP-196 encoding, 128 bytes, and returns their sum, 64 bytes; `eip196Eip197MulG1` takes a first-group point and a scalar, 96 bytes, and the scalar field's order, and returns the product, 64 bytes; `eip196Eip197PairingCheck` takes one or more pairs of a first-group point and a second-group point in the EIP-197 encoding, a positive multiple of 192 bytes, and returns whether the product of the pairings is one; `eip196Eip197CheckG2` takes a second-group point, 128 bytes, and returns when the point is in the subgroup of order `r` and reverts otherwise; `eip196Eip197CheckScalar` takes a scalar and the scalar field's order and returns for a scalar below the order and reverts otherwise
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the EIP-2537 set: `eip2537AddG1` takes two first-group points in the EIP-2537 encoding, 256 bytes, and `eip2537AddG2` two second-group points, 512 bytes, each refusing an operand outside the subgroup and returning the sum, 128 and 256 bytes; `eip2537MulG1` takes a first-group point and a scalar, 160 bytes, and the scalar field's order, and returns the product; `eip2537MsmG1` and `eip2537MsmG2` take a positive multiple of 160 and of 288 bytes, one point and one scalar per term, and the scalar field's order, and return the sum; `eip2537PairingCheck` takes a positive multiple of 384 bytes and returns whether the product of the pairings is one; `eip2537CheckG1` and `eip2537CheckG2` take a point, 128 and 256 bytes, and return when it is in the subgroup and revert otherwise; `eip2537CheckScalar` takes a scalar and the scalar field's order and returns for a scalar below the order and reverts otherwise
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a scalar a multiplication or a multi-scalar multiplication takes is refused when it is not below the scalar field order the caller passes, whatever the precompile accepts, so the library refuses the scalar field's order itself as the non-canonical scalar the rejection vectors enumerate
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a precompile that fails reverts the call with `PrecompileFailed` carrying the precompile's address; a fixed-size operand of the wrong length reverts with `EncodingLength` carrying the expected and the actual length; a variable input that is empty or not a multiple of its unit reverts with `InputLength` carrying the unit and the actual length; a refused scalar reverts with `ScalarNotCanonical` carrying the scalar
    * `[ ]`   Functional: every operation's result for the committed vector files of every cell directory equals the record's output, and every candidate in the committed decode-rejection vectors is refused
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the library holds no state, is `internal` throughout, makes no call but the precompile `staticcall`, and names no generated symbol; every precompile address and byte length is a named private constant whose source is EIP-196, EIP-197, or EIP-2537, and every scalar field order arrives as the argument the caller passes

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the registry accepts a credential delivery only when the delivery verifier accepts its proof, and the verifier must accept exactly what `proof/schnorr_fs` accepts, so the contract recomputes the transcript's challenge under the same encoding, tag, and scalar field, the same first messages, and the same weights and pairing product, and refuses every statement and proof the reference refuses (CD-03; CR-09; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier` row; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and exit for `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier`; the technical requirements' `DeliveryVerifier` entry and the Delivery proof section's verifier statements)

    * `[ ]`   Functional: `verifyTransfer` returns true exactly when the statement's purpose is `PURPOSE_TRANSFER` or `PURPOSE_GRANT` and the transfer relation's challenge check, and in the first-group-only form its hash-weighted pairing product, hold
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a statement whose purpose proves the other relation returns false before any other step; a proof of the other verifier form, recognised by the length of its `secondGroupFirstMessages`, returns false
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a statement member that is not one point of its group's encoded length, a challenge or response that is not below the scalar field order, and `secondGroupFirstMessages` of a length that is neither empty nor three second-group points revert with a custom error, because the reference refuses them at its decoding boundary and never returns a verdict for them
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the contract is constructed with the verifier's precompile set, the scalar field's order, and the generators' encodings, declares the delivery-statement version it verifies through `statementVersion`, and declares its verifier form through `secondGroupArithmetic`, true for the both-groups form
    * `[ ]`   Functional: every mint and transfer vector of each cell directory's committed vector files is accepted by a verifier constructed from that directory's values, and a statement or proof differing from a vector in one context field, one group element, one response, the challenge, or one second-group first message is refused

    * `[ ]`   Relation gate, `verifyMint`: condition `context.purpose` is neither `PURPOSE_MINT` nor `PURPOSE_REPLACEMENT`; decision comparison, before any other step; dependency call none; outcome return false. `verifyTransfer`: the same with `PURPOSE_TRANSFER` and `PURPOSE_GRANT`

    * `[ ]`   Member groups, `verifyMint`: `hpub` second, `identityElement` first, `buyerPk1` first, `buyerPk2` second, `envelopeC1` first, `envelopeC2` first, `envelopeD1` second, `envelopeD2` second; `verifyTransfer`: `identityElement` first, `sellerPk1` first, `sellerPk2` second, `buyerPk1` first, `buyerPk2` second, and for the old and then the new envelope `C1` first, `C2` first, `D1` second, `D2` second
    * `[ ]`   Scalar guard: condition the challenge or a response is not below the constructed scalar field order; decision the constructed set's `eip196Eip197CheckScalar` or `eip2537CheckScalar`, the challenge first and then the responses in the proof's member order, after the member guard; dependency call that function; outcome the library's revert `ScalarNotCanonical(scalar)`
    * `[ ]`   First-message guard: condition `secondGroupFirstMessages` has a length other than zero and three times the constructed set's second-group encoded length; decision length comparison, after the scalar guard; dependency call none; outcome revert `SecondGroupFirstMessagesLength(actual)`
    * `[ ]`   Form gate: condition the length of `secondGroupFirstMessages` is not the constructed form's, three second-group points for the first-group-only form and zero for the both-groups form; decision length comparison, after the first-message guard; dependency call none; outcome return false
    * `[ ]`   Scalar arithmetic: every scalar operation is modulo the constructed scalar field order `q`, a sum by `addmod`, a product by `mulmod`, and the negation of `x` is `(q - x) % q`; `n` is the negation of the challenge `c`
    * `[ ]`   Combination: a combination of first-group points and scalars is one `eip2537MsmG1` over the terms, each a point followed by its scalar and the order `q` as the `order` argument, on the `Eip2537` set, and on the `Eip196Eip197` set the sum, by `eip196Eip197AddG1`, of the `eip196Eip197MulG1` of each term under the order `q`; a combination of second-group points is one `eip2537MsmG2` over the terms and occurs on the `Eip2537` set alone

    * `[ ]`   Hash to scalar: `uint256(keccak256(abi.encodePacked(uint8(tag.length), tag, message))) % q`, the challenge under `CHALLENGE_TAG` over the transcript, a weight under `WEIGHT_TAG` over `abi.encodePacked` of the challenge, every response in the proof's member order, and the position byte as a `uint8`, one for `w2` and two for `w3`
    * `[ ]`   Mint first messages, `c` the challenge and `zα`, `zr`, `zρ`, `zσ` the responses `alpha`, `r`, `rho`, `sigma`, with `g1` and `g2` the constructed generator encodings and `F` the `identityElement`: `T_C1` is the first-group combination of `(g1, zρ)` and `(envelopeC1, n)`; `T_C2` of `(g1, zα)`, `(F, zr)`, `(buyerPk1, zρ)`, and `(envelopeC2, n)`; in the both-groups form `T_hpub` is the second-group combination of `(g2, zα)` and `(hpub, n)`, `T_D1` of `(g2, zσ)` and `(envelopeD1, n)`, and `T_D2` of `(g2, zr)`, `(buyerPk2, zσ)`, and `(envelopeD2, n)`; in the first-group-only form `T_hpub`, `T_D1`, and `T_D2` are the three second-group points of `secondGroupFirstMessages`, in that order
    * `[ ]`   Transfer first messages, `zx`, `zy`, `zs`, `zρ`, `zσ` the responses `x`, `y`, `s`, `rho`, `sigma`, with `F` the `identityElement`: `T_pk1` is the first-group combination of `(g1, zx)` and `(sellerPk1, n)`; `T_C1` of `(g1, zρ)` and `(newC1, n)`; `T_C2` of `(oldC1, -zx)`, `(F, zs)`, `(buyerPk1, zρ)`, `(newC2, n)`, and `(oldC2, c)`; in the both-groups form `T_pk2` is the second-group combination of `(g2, zy)` and `(sellerPk2, n)`, `T_D1` of `(g2, zσ)` and `(newD1, n)`, and `T_D2` of `(oldD1, -zy)`, `(g2, zs)`, `(buyerPk2, zσ)`, `(newD2, n)`, and `(oldD2, c)`; in the first-group-only form `T_pk2`, `T_D1`, and `T_D2` are the three second-group points of `secondGroupFirstMessages`, in that order
    * `[ ]`   The term `(oldC2, c)` and `(oldD2, c)` is the negation of `n` applied to the old second element, the reference's `ΔC2` and `ΔD2` times `n` taken as `n` times the new element plus the negation of `n` times the old element, and the negation of `n` is `c`

    * `[ ]`   Weighted product, first-group-only form only, after the challenge check holds: `w2` and `w3` by the hash to scalar, and `eip196Eip197PairingCheck` over the pairs of a first-group point and a second-group point, each first-group point `e` times `g1` by `eip196Eip197MulG1` under the order `q`; the mint pairs are `(q-1, T_hpub)`, `(-w2, T_D1)`, `(-w3, T_D2)`, `(zα + w2·zσ + w3·zr, g2)`, `(n, hpub)`, `(w2·n, envelopeD1)`, `(w3·zσ, buyerPk2)`, and `(w3·n, envelopeD2)`; the transfer pairs are `(q-1, T_pk2)`, `(-w2, T_D1)`, `(-w3, T_D2)`, `(zy + w2·zσ + w3·zs, g2)`, `(n, sellerPk2)`, `(w2·n, newD1)`, `(-(w3·zy), oldD1)`, `(w3·zσ, buyerPk2)`, `(w3·n, newD2)`, and `(w3·c, oldD2)`; outcome return the check's verdict
    * `[ ]`   Accepted, both-groups form: the challenge check holds; outcome return true
    * `[ ]`   Ordering: the relation gate, the member guard, the scalar guard, the first-message guard, the form gate, the first messages, the transcript and the challenge check, and in the first-group-only form the weights and the pairing check; the first refusal is returned; no state is read or written

* `[ ]`   `contracts/evm/IdentityAccount` **The identity's contract account: its signer set is its device registry, keyed by scheme-tagged key identifiers and verified through the signature verifier it is constructed over; device admission, re-scoping, and revocation as signed intents; the `isDeviceAdmitted` view; the intent nonce; ERC-1271 verification; and the verification of a signed intent, EIP-712 typed data over the call's fields, chain, contract, nonce, and expiry, against a signer whose permissions cover the call; authors the signature verifier's interface `ISignatureVerifier` with its ABI description, interface test, and mock, and its own interface, permission vocabulary, ABI description, interface test, mock, guard test, unit test, and integration test**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: every function that mutates identity-bound state takes the acting identity and a signed intent verified through the identity's account against a signer whose permissions cover the call, so a paymaster-sponsored call, a relayer-submitted call, and a self-funded call share one entry point; the account therefore holds the identity's signer set, refuses foreign, expired, replayed, revoked-signer, and out-of-permission intents, and answers whether a key is an admitted device in the same state view as the authorization (LC-13; IW-05; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/IdentityAccount` row; the technical requirements' Identity paragraph and its `DeviceSigner` and `IntentNonce` records; MVP Scope's Key Custody paragraphs on multi-device identity; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope and exit for the identity account)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the account's signer set is its device registry, one `DeviceSigner` record per key identifier holding `scheme`, `role`, `permissions`, `admittedAt`, and `revokedAt`; a key identifier is the Keccak-256 digest of the bytes of the generated constants entry `KEY_IDENTIFIER_DOMAIN_TAG`, the scheme's one byte, and the key's bytes, in that order, the derivation the chain forms' declaration states and `generated/Constants.sol` emits; a signer is admitted when `admittedAt` is nonzero and `revokedAt` is zero
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `permissions` is a `uint256` bitmask over the account's permission vocabulary, one bit per identity-bound operation the technical requirements' Identity paragraph lists, in that order: `bindHandshakeKey`, `admitDevice`, `rescopeDevice`, `revokeDevice`, `registerEnvelopeKeys`, `requestGrants`, `withdrawRequests`, `grant`, `replace`, `mint`, `lock`, and `deliver`, bit zero through bit eleven; a signer's permissions cover a call when every bit the call requires is set in the signer's `permissions`; `role` is a `uint8` the account stores and returns and never interprets, the roles being the custody adapter's versioned capabilities
    * `[ ]`   Functional: control authority, the authority over binding and the device set, is the holding of the control permissions, `bindHandshakeKey`, `admitDevice`, `rescopeDevice`, and `revokeDevice`; the signer the account is constructed with holds exactly the control permissions, being the bound handshake key, and the permissions of every other device are those its admission assigns
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a signed intent is verified as EIP-712 typed data whose digest is the one the chain forms' signed-intent form returns: the domain the generated entry `INTENT_DOMAIN_NAME` names at the generated entry `INTENT_DOMAIN_VERSION`, the chain identifier as `chainId`, and the contract the call is addressed to as `verifyingContract`; the struct the generated entry `INTENT_STRUCT_NAME` names, holding the acting identity as the generated entry `FIXED_BYTES20_TYPE_NAME`, the nonce as `NONCE_KIND_TYPE_NAME`, the expiry as `EXPIRY_KIND_TYPE_NAME`, and a nested struct named by the operation whose members are the call's named fields in order, each typed by the generated `<KIND>_TYPE_NAME` entry of its canonical kind; the acting identity is this account's address
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the acting signer is named by the intent's scheme and key, whose key identifier must be an admitted signer of the account; the signature is verified through `ISignatureVerifier.isValidSignature(scheme, key, message, signature)` with the 32-byte digest as the message, so the account names no scheme, no key format, and no signature algorithm, and a scheme bound after the account exists is verified by the verifier the account was constructed over with no change to the account
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `verifyIntent`, called by the contract the call is addressed to, refuses an intent whose expiry has passed, whose nonce is not the account's next nonce, whose signer is not admitted or is revoked, whose signer's permissions do not cover the permissions the caller requires, or whose signature the verifier refuses, and on success advances the nonce by one and returns the signer's key identifier
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `admitDevice`, `rescopeDevice`, and `revokeDevice` are each a signed intent addressed to the account itself, verified by the same checks, covered by a signer holding the operation's permission, the permissions an admission or re-scoping assigns being the signer's to assign within the vocabulary; a key identifier once admitted is recorded permanently, so a revoked key identifier is never admitted again
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `isDeviceAdmitted(identity, keyId)` returns true exactly when `identity` is this account and `keyId` names an admitted signer, a view read in the same state view as any authorization the caller evaluates
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `isValidSignature(hash, signature)` is ERC-1271: `signature` is the encoding of the signer's scheme, key, and signature, the result is the magic value `0x1626ba7e` exactly when the signer is admitted, covers the control permissions, and the verifier accepts the signature over the 32-byte `hash`, and `0xffffffff` otherwise
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/abi/IIdentityAccount.json` declares the interface's functions, events, and errors, and `contracts/evm/src/IIdentityAccount.sol` realizes them in Solidity with the same names, order, and types
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the interface is stated in one description and one realization, neither copied into another file; the interface owns the permission vocabulary, the builders, and the mock that stand in for it; the account holds no storage but the signer records, the nonce, and its immutable verifier, and calls nothing but `ISignatureVerifier`

    * `[ ]`   `IdentityAccount` is a contract whose branch is selected by its inputs and its stored signer records, never by the caller's identity: the key identifier, the intent digest, the permission coverage, and the signature check are the same whoever submits
    * `[ ]`   Key identifier: the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encodePacked(Constants.KEY_IDENTIFIER_DOMAIN_TAG, bytes1(scheme), key)`
    * `[ ]`   Intent digest, for an `operation`, a `callType`, a `callHash`, a `verifyingContract`, and the intent's `nonce` and `expiry`: the intent's type digest is the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encodePacked(Constants.INTENT_STRUCT_NAME, "(", Constants.FIXED_BYTES20_TYPE_NAME, " ", Constants.INTENT_MEMBER_IDENTITY, ",", Constants.NONCE_KIND_TYPE_NAME, " ", Constants.INTENT_MEMBER_NONCE, ",", Constants.EXPIRY_KIND_TYPE_NAME, " ", Constants.INTENT_MEMBER_EXPIRY, ",", operation, " call)", callType)`; the intent's hash is the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encode(intentTypeDigest, bytes32(bytes20(address(this))), uint256(nonce), uint256(expiry), callHash)`; the domain separator is the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encode(keccak256("EIP712Domain(string name,string version,uint256 chainId,address verifyingContract)"), keccak256(bytes(Constants.INTENT_DOMAIN_NAME)), keccak256(bytes(Constants.INTENT_DOMAIN_VERSION)), block.chainid, verifyingContract)`; the digest is the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encodePacked(hex"1901", domainSeparator, intentHash)`
    * `[ ]`   Call hash of an own operation, with `callTypeDigest` the Keccak-256 digest of the call type string, the call type assembled from the operation's name and each member's generated type-name entry and generated member name: `admitDevice` has the call type `admitDevice(<UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME> scheme,<FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME> keyId,<UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME> role,<UNSIGNED256_TYPE_NAME> permissions)` and the call hash `keccak256(abi.encode(callTypeDigest, uint256(scheme), keyId, uint256(role), permissions))`; `rescopeDevice` has `rescopeDevice(<FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME> keyId,<UNSIGNED256_TYPE_NAME> permissions)` and `keccak256(abi.encode(callTypeDigest, keyId, permissions))`; `revokeDevice` has `revokeDevice(<FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME> keyId)` and `keccak256(abi.encode(callTypeDigest, keyId))`; each operation name is the function's name, each kind named by the `Constants` type-name entry for its canonical kind, a scheme and a role each under `Constants.UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME`
    * `[ ]`   Constructor guards, in order: condition the verifier is the zero address; outcome revert `VerifierUnset()`; condition the initial scheme is zero; outcome revert `SchemeUnassigned()`; each decision a comparison before any state is written; otherwise the initial signer is recorded under its key identifier with `permissions` `PERMISSIONS_CONTROL`, `admittedAt` the block's timestamp and `revokedAt` zero, and `DeviceAdmitted` is emitted
    * `[ ]`   Intent verification, `verifyIntent` and the verification each device operation performs, with `verifyingContract` the caller for `verifyIntent` and this account for a device operation, the checks in this order and the first refusal returned: condition `block.timestamp` is greater than the intent's `expiry`; outcome revert `IntentExpired(expiry, block.timestamp)`; condition the intent's `nonce` is not the stored nonce; outcome revert `IntentNonceMismatch(stored, given)`; condition the signer record of the derived key identifier has `admittedAt` zero; outcome revert `SignerNotAdmitted(keyId)`; condition the record's `revokedAt` is nonzero; outcome revert `SignerRevoked(keyId)`; condition `requiredPermissions` has a bit not set in the record's `permissions`; outcome revert `PermissionsNotCovered(keyId, requiredPermissions, permissions)`; condition `ISignatureVerifier.isValidSignature(scheme, key, abi.encodePacked(digest), signature)` returns false; dependency call that function on the constructed verifier; outcome revert `SignatureInvalid(keyId)`; otherwise the stored nonce advances by one and the key identifier is returned
    * `[ ]`   `admitDevice`: guards, before intent verification and in order, condition `scheme` is zero, outcome revert `SchemeUnassigned()`, and condition `permissions` sets a bit outside `PERMISSIONS_ALL`, outcome revert `PermissionsOutsideVocabulary(permissions)`; verification with required permissions `PERMISSION_ADMIT_DEVICE`; condition the record of `keyId` has `admittedAt` nonzero, admitted or revoked; outcome revert `SignerAlreadyRecorded(keyId)`; otherwise the record is stored with `scheme`, `role`, `permissions`, `admittedAt` the block's timestamp, and `revokedAt` zero, and `DeviceAdmitted` is emitted
    * `[ ]`   `rescopeDevice`: guard condition `permissions` sets a bit outside `PERMISSIONS_ALL`, outcome revert `PermissionsOutsideVocabulary(permissions)`; verification with required permissions `PERMISSION_RESCOPE_DEVICE`; condition the record of `keyId` has `admittedAt` zero, outcome revert `SignerNotAdmitted(keyId)`; condition its `revokedAt` is nonzero, outcome revert `SignerRevoked(keyId)`; otherwise the record's `permissions` is replaced and `DeviceRescoped` is emitted
    * `[ ]`   `revokeDevice`: verification with required permissions `PERMISSION_REVOKE_DEVICE`; condition the record of `keyId` has `admittedAt` zero, outcome revert `SignerNotAdmitted(keyId)`; condition its `revokedAt` is nonzero, outcome revert `SignerRevoked(keyId)`; otherwise `revokedAt` is the block's timestamp and `DeviceRevoked` is emitted
    * `[ ]`   `isDeviceAdmitted`: returns true when `identity` is this account's address and the record of `keyId` has `admittedAt` nonzero and `revokedAt` zero, and false otherwise; reads state only
    * `[ ]`   `deviceSigner` returns the record of `keyId`, every member zero for an unrecorded key identifier; `intentNonce` returns the stored nonce
    * `[ ]`   `isValidSignature`: `signature` is decoded as `(uint8 scheme, bytes key, bytes innerSignature)`; condition the record of the derived key identifier is not admitted, or its permissions do not include every bit of `PERMISSIONS_CONTROL`, or `ISignatureVerifier.isValidSignature(scheme, key, abi.encodePacked(hash), innerSignature)` returns false; outcome return `bytes4(0xffffffff)`; otherwise return `bytes4(0x1626ba7e)`; reads state only
    * `[ ]`   Ordering: a device operation's argument guards precede its intent verification, the intent verification precedes every state write, and the nonce advances only on the success path; a revert leaves the nonce and every record unchanged

    * `[ ]`   Identity address: the CREATE2 address under `address(this)`, with salt `keyCommitment`, of `type(IdentityAccount).creationCode` followed by `abi.encode(signatureVerifier, scheme, key, role)`, the creation code's hash being the Keccak-256 digest of that concatenation; `identityAddress` returns it and `bindHandshakeKey` creates the account at it
    * `[ ]`   Call type and call hash of the binding, with `callTypeDigest` the Keccak-256 digest of the call type string: the call type is `bindHandshakeKey(` followed by `Constants.UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME`, ` scheme,`, `Constants.FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME`, ` keyCommitment,`, `Constants.FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME`, ` anchorHash,`, `Constants.UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME`, and ` role)`, assembled with `string.concat`; the call hash is `keccak256(abi.encode(callTypeDigest, uint256(scheme), keyCommitment, anchorHash, uint256(role)))`; the operation name is `bindHandshakeKey`, the function's name, and a scheme and a role are each under `Constants.UNSIGNED16_TYPE_NAME`, as `IdentityAccount`'s own operations state
    * `[ ]`   `bindHandshakeKey`, the checks in this order and the first refusal returned: condition the binding record of the derived identity holds a nonzero `handshakeKeyCommitment`; decision a comparison before any account is created; outcome revert `IdentityAlreadyBound(identity)`; dependency call `new IdentityAccount{salt: keyCommitment}(signatureVerifier, scheme, key, role)`, whose constructor guards, `VerifierUnset` and `SchemeUnassigned`, revert through this call unchanged with their full data; condition `IIdentityAccount(identity).isDeviceAdmitted(identity, keyCommitment)` returns false; dependency call that function on the created account; outcome revert `HandshakeKeyCommitmentMismatch(keyCommitment)`; dependency call `IIdentityAccount(identity).verifyIntent(intent, "bindHandshakeKey", callType, callHash, PERMISSION_BIND_HANDSHAKE_KEY)`, whose refusals this call reaches, `IntentExpired`, `IntentNonceMismatch`, `SignerNotAdmitted`, and `SignatureInvalid`, revert through this call unchanged with their full data, the account being new and its initial signer holding every control permission, so `SignerRevoked` and `PermissionsNotCovered` are never reached; otherwise the record `{handshakeKeyCommitment: keyCommitment, scheme: scheme, anchorHash: anchorHash}` is stored under `identity`, `HandshakeKeyBound(identity, scheme, keyCommitment, anchorHash)` is emitted, and `identity` is returned
    * `[ ]`   `bindingOf`: returns the stored record of `identity`, every member zero for an identity no call bound; reads state only
    * `[ ]`   `identityAddress`: returns the address the interaction spec's identity address states for the inputs it is handed, whether or not an account exists there; reads state only
    * `[ ]`   Ordering: the already-bound check precedes the account's creation, the commitment check precedes the intent verification, the intent verification precedes the record's write, and the record is written and the event emitted only on the success path; a revert at any step discards the created account and leaves no record, so an identity holds a binding record exactly when its account exists and verified the intent that bound it
    * `[ ]`   The boundary is crossed by this node's integration test, `contracts/evm/test/Binding.integration.t.sol`; the Rust integration elements do not apply to a contract

* `[ ]`   `contracts/evm/Registry` **The suite's registry: asset records keyed by the asset's root with append-only alias records beside them, deployments admitted only with a sidecar for every parameter set live at registration and a verified release attestation or a recorded absence, sidecar addition for a later set, the parameter sets and source keys under the governance key, durable batched grant requests, and the three-state `evaluateAuthorization` with its batch; constructed over the bindings the adapter registry injects, creating the `ParameterSets` it reads; authors the interfaces `IRegistry`, `IEntitlement`, `IIdentityAdapter`, and `IAttestationVerifier`, the ABI descriptions of the last two, and each interface's builders, mock, and interface test, and its own interaction spec, guard test, unit test, and integration test**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the registry is the ledger the protocol's decentralized properties rest on, so one contract holds the asset's identity and its alias records, admits a deployment only when its hash-card covers every live parameter set and its release attestation verifies or is recorded absent, holds the grant requests a first run registers, and answers every attempt context with one of three states, and every identity-bound call it takes is a signed intent verified through the identity's account (LC-01; LC-04 through the three-state result; LC-06; LC-07; LC-09; LC-12; LC-13; the dependency map's `contracts/evm/Registry` row and its Registry ownership list; the technical requirements' Registry, Requests, Authorization, and Identity paragraphs and their `Asset`, `Deployment`, `AliasRecord`, `SourceKey`, and `Request` records; MVP Scope's Entitlement Ledger and Escrow Contract; the Registry and entitlement contracts milestone's scope and exit; the research's Abstract Identity Adapter and Per-Attempt Authorization sections)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `registerAsset(root, namespace, key, statement, absent, proofClass)` records the asset keyed by `root` once, with the caller as its authority and issuance authority, the bound entitlement as its entitlement adapter, and `proofClass`, and appends one alias entry holding `root` and either the source's signed `statement` or its recorded absence to the list of the alias key `key` under `namespace`; no entry is rebound or removed, and an asset registered once is never registered again, so finders holding the same bytes reach one asset (MVP Scope, Entitlement Ledger and Escrow Contract: the asset record registers the publisher's authority and issuance authority; cryptography.md, Abstract Identity Adapter: the identity adapter answers whether a proved identity may register)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the alias key is interpreted by no registry field; the alias list is addressed by the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encode(namespace, key)`, and `resolve(namespace, key)` returns the entries in append order while `resolveBatch(namespaces, keys)` returns one list per pair in the order given and agrees with `resolve` for each pair
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `registerDeployment(asset, record, attestation, attestationAbsent)` admits `record` only for a registered asset and a caller the bound identity adapter authorizes to register it, only when `record.sidecars` holds exactly one sidecar for each parameter set live for the asset and none for any other set, and only when the release attestation verifies through the attestation verifier bound for the asset's namespace, against the source key the attestation names and the table holds active, or `attestationAbsent` is true and `attestation` is empty; it assigns `deploymentId`, appends it to the asset's deployments, and returns it, so a later First Finder holding the same bytes registers a further deployment of the same asset; an attestation is the source's key identifier, for npm the 32-byte SHA-256 fingerprint the `keyid` of a `dist.signatures` entry carries, followed by the source's P-256 signature over `name@version:integrity` (npm, About registry signatures; the technical requirements' `SourceKey` record keyed by `keyId bytes32`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the deployment id is the Keccak-256 digest of `abi.encode(address(this), block.chainid, asset, the asset's deployment count before the append)`, unique across assets, chains, and registries and never reused
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `addSidecar(deployment, parameterSetId, root, locator)` is callable only by the asset's authority, adds a sidecar for a parameter set that is live for the deployment's asset and that the deployment does not already carry, and never alters a sidecar registered with the deployment
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `registerParameterSet` and `retireParameterSet`, `rotateSourceKey` and `revokeSourceKey` are callable only by the governance address the registry is constructed with; registering a set records it in `ParameterSets` and marks it live for its asset, and retiring a set is refused while `IEntitlement.liveEntitlementCountOf` for the set is nonzero (LC-09)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a source key is a `SourceKey` record holding `sourceId`, `keyId`, `key`, `activeFrom`, and `revokedAt`, recorded once per `(sourceId, keyId)`; a key is active at a timestamp when `activeFrom` is at most that timestamp and `revokedAt` is zero or above it; revocation sets `revokedAt` to the block's timestamp and keeps the record
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `requestGrants(identity, assets, intent)` and `withdrawRequests(identity, assets, intent)` each record or close one request per asset for `identity`, batched, after the intent is verified through the account at `identity` with the permission `PERMISSION_REQUEST_GRANTS` and `PERMISSION_WITHDRAW_REQUESTS` respectively; a request holds `openedAt`, `closedAt`, and `closedBy`, a `uint8` that is `0` while the request is open, `1` when a grant closed it, and `2` when a withdrawal closed it, and an open request authorizes nothing; `openRequests(asset, page)` returns a page of the identities holding an open request for `asset` and the next page, zero when none follows; `closeRequestByGrant(identity, asset)` is callable only by the bound entitlement contract, closes the open request of `identity` for `asset` with `closedBy` `1` when one is open, and changes nothing otherwise, so a grant to an identity with no open request settles unaffected (LC-12; the technical requirements' `grant` closing the recipient's open request)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the call type of each request operation is the operation's name, a parenthesis, the generated `Constants.FIXED_BYTES32_TYPE_NAME` with the EIP-712 array suffix `[]`, ` assets`, and a parenthesis, and the call hash is `keccak256(abi.encode(callTypeDigest, keccak256(abi.encodePacked(assets))))`, so the signature binds every asset named and the order, and a relayer cannot change any of them
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `evaluateAuthorization(context)` returns `AUTHORIZED`, `PENDING_SETTLEMENT`, or `DENIED`, as the values `0`, `1`, and `2`, from the context's every field: `DENIED` for an entitlement contract other than the bound one, an entitlement of another asset than `context.asset`, a deployment that does not belong to that asset, an issuance authority the identity adapter does not authorize to mint, an identity whose binding record does not carry `context.handshakeKeyScheme` and `context.handshakeKeyCommitment`, a token interval above `context.interval`, and an owner other than `context.identity`; `PENDING_SETTLEMENT` for a token interval below `context.interval` or an entitlement the token does not hold at the read reference; `AUTHORIZED` otherwise; the view is read at the block the context's settlement reference selects, so a lower interval or an absent token is the context's own settlement not yet at that tier, and a higher interval is a credential superseded (cryptography.md, Per-Attempt Authorization and its result table; Abstract Identity Adapter on the interval index)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `evaluateAuthorizationBatch(contexts, page)` returns the results for the window of `contexts` that starts at `page` times the registry's page limit and spans at most that limit, each equal to `evaluateAuthorization` of its context and in the order given, and the next page, zero when none follows; a nonzero page over no contexts, and a page whose window starts at or beyond a nonempty list, are refused, and no contexts at page zero return no results and a next page of zero (LC-06)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the registry reaches the entitlement through `IEntitlement`, the issuance lineage through `IIdentityAdapter`, the release attestation through `IAttestationVerifier`, and the binding record through `IBinding`, each constructor-injected and immutable, and names no token standard, no attestation scheme, and no signature algorithm
    * `[ ]`   Functional: `contracts/evm/src/IRegistry.sol` and `contracts/evm/src/IEntitlement.sol` realize `contracts/evm/abi/Registry.json` and `contracts/evm/abi/IEntitlement.json`, which `chain/base` authors, with the same names, order, and types; `contracts/evm/abi/IIdentityAdapter.json` and `contracts/evm/abi/IAttestationVerifier.json` declare the interfaces of the same names, and each interface is realized in Solidity with the same names, order, and types
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: each interface is stated in one description and one realization, neither copied into another file; the registry names no generated symbol other than the type-name entries its call types read, holds no volume limit, and calls only the four bound interfaces, `IIdentityAccount.verifyIntent`, and the `ParameterSets` it created

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   Contract suite contract: a Solidity contract of `contracts/evm` implementing `IRegistry`, constructed over the governance address, `IBinding`, `IEntitlement`, `IIdentityAdapter`, the attestation verifier per namespace, and the batch page limit, and creating the `ParameterSets` whose registry address is its own
    * `[ ]`   Owns the interfaces `contracts/evm/src/IRegistry.sol`, `contracts/evm/src/IEntitlement.sol`, `contracts/evm/src/IIdentityAdapter.sol`, and `contracts/evm/src/IAttestationVerifier.sol`, with the ABI descriptions of the last two, their builders, mocks, and interface tests, its custom errors, its events, its structs, and its records
    * `[ ]`   Has no provides file of its own: the public ABI is the provides
    * `[ ]`   Does not implement an entitlement token, an identity adapter, an attestation verifier, an envelope-key registry, an escrow, a claim, or a verifier-key record; does not verify a delivery proof, move a payment, or bind an adapter; does not interpret suite-tagged material, a statement, or an alias key
    * `[ ]`   Does not create `contracts/evm`, `foundry.toml`, or `.github/workflows/contracts.yml`; `contracts/evm/ParameterSets` created them
    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit

  * `[ ]`   `module`
    * `[ ]`   Bounded context: `contracts/evm`, holding `src/IRegistry.sol`, `src/IEntitlement.sol`, `src/IIdentityAdapter.sol`, `src/IAttestationVerifier.sol`, `abi/IIdentityAdapter.json`, `abi/IAttestationVerifier.json`, `src/Registry.interaction.spec.md`, `src/Registry.sol`, the interface tests `test/Registry.interface.t.sol`, `test/Entitlement.interface.t.sol`, `test/IdentityAdapter.interface.t.sol`, and `test/AttestationVerifier.interface.t.sol`, the mocks `test/RegistryMock.sol`, `test/EntitlementMock.sol`, `test/IdentityAdapterMock.sol`, and `test/AttestationVerifierMock.sol`, and `test/Registry.guard.t.sol`, `test/Registry.t.sol`, and `test/Registry.integration.t.sol`

  * `[ ]`   `contracts/evm/src/Registry.interaction.spec.md`
    * `[ ]`   `Registry` is a contract whose branch is selected by its inputs and its stored records, never by the caller's identity except where a function names the caller it admits: the alias key, the call type, the call hash, and the authorization result are the same whoever submits
